✅ Status: FULLY CORRECT

🤖 archi's Answer
For large datasets you don't need backed up, the best option on the FASRC Cannon cluster is **netscratch** at **`/n/netscratch`** [Getting Started with FASRC Storage](https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/getting-started-with-fasrc-storage/). Key details about netscratch: - **Path:** `/n/netscratch` - **Capacity:** 50TB per PI/lab account (complimentary) - **Backup:** No backups (snapshots only) - **Retention policy:** 90-day retention — files older than 90 days may be automatically cleaned up - **Purpose:** Designed as temporary storage for high-performance data analysis and I/O-intensive workloads This is ideal for large intermediate datasets, temporary working files, or data you can regenerate. If you need longer-term retention without backups, your **Lab Directory** at `/n/holylabs` (4TB per lab, no backups but no automatic retention policy) is another option, though it has a much smaller capacity. For reference, your **Home Directory** (`/n/homeNN/`) is also not backed up (daily snapshots retained for 2 weeks), but it's limited to 100GB and not recommended for large datasets or I/O-intensive jobs [Home and Lab directories – FASRC DOCS](https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/cluster-storage/).
✅ Expected Answer
Use /n/holyscratch01// for fast scratch storage. It is not backed up and is periodically purged of older files.

Question 108: question_108

❓ Question

My SLURM job on Cannon was killed with an out-of-memory error. How do I diagnose what the actual memory peak was, and how do I request more memory for the next submission?

🎯 Retrieval Check

Expected Document(s): https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/running-jobs

Retrieved Documents: docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/faq, docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/running-jobs, docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/running-jobs, slurm.schedmd.com/slurm.conf.html, docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/slurm-stats, slurm.schedmd.com/faq.html, docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/slurm-memory-limits, slurm.schedmd.com/faq.html

✅ Status: FULLY CORRECT

🤖 archi's Answer
Here's how to diagnose and fix the OOM issue on Cannon: ## Diagnosing the memory peak Slurm only records an **accurate** memory peak if the job completes successfully. If your job was killed with `OUT_OF_MEMORY`, the recorded memory usage will be inaccurate. To find the true peak: 1. **Run a test job** with the same workload but with a generous memory request so it completes without being killed. 2. **Check the recorded peak** using `sacct`: ``` sacct -j --format=JobID,MaxRSS ``` (MaxRSS shows the peak resident set size in KB.) 3. You can also use the **`jobstats`** tool to analyze how much memory your job actually used ([Slurm memory limits](https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/slurm-memory-limits/)). ## Requesting more memory Add a `--mem` directive to your `#SBATCH` block: ```bash #SBATCH --mem 4G ``` This requests 4 GB per node. You can use `M` for megabytes or `G` for gigabytes. **Tip:** Start with a generous request (job slots average ~4000 MB per core), then use `jobstats` or `sacct` to see what you actually used, and tighten the request. Smaller requests are easier for the scheduler to place. For more details, see [Slurm memory limits](https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/slurm-memory-limits/).
✅ Expected Answer
After the job ends, run sacct -j --format=JobID,MaxRSS,ReqMem to see the peak memory the job used (MaxRSS) versus what was requested (ReqMem). In your next submit script, increase the memory request via --mem=G (whole-job) or --mem-per-cpu=G (per-task), aiming a bit above the observed MaxRSS so future variations don't kill the job again.
🎯 Expected Source Documents
  • https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/running-jobs

Here's how to diagnose and fix the OOM issue on Cannon: ## Diagnosing the memory peak Slurm only records an **accurate** memory peak if the job completes successfully. If your job was killed with `OUT_OF_MEMORY`, the recorded memory usage will be inaccurate. To find the true peak: 1. **Run a test job** with the same workload but with a generous memory request so it completes without being killed. 2. **Check the recorded peak** using `sacct`: ``` sacct -j <job_id> --format=JobID,MaxRSS ``` (MaxRSS shows the peak resident set size in KB.) 3. You can also use the **`jobstats`** tool to analyze how much memory your job actually used ([Slurm memory limits](https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/slurm-memory-limits/)). ## Requesting more memory Add a `--mem` directive to your `#SBATCH` block: ```bash #SBATCH --mem 4G ``` This requests 4 GB per node. Smaller requests are easier for the scheduler to place. For more details, see [Slurm memory limits](https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/slurm-memory-limits/).

Question 109: question_109

❓ Question

I want to run a multi-node MPI program on Cannon that uses one GPU per node. What does the SLURM submit script need to specify, and how do I launch the MPI ranks?

🎯 Retrieval Check

Expected Document(s): https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/running-jobs, https://docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/mpi-message-passing-interface

Retrieved Documents: github.com/fasrc/User_Codes/blob/master/Training/Distributed_GPU_Computing/Exercise6/run.sbatch, github.com/fasrc/User_Codes/blob/master/GPU_Computing/Dist_GPU_Computing/NCCL_and_CUDA/MultiNode/README.md, docs.rc.fas.harvard.edu/kb/running-jobs, github.com/fasrc/User_Codes/blob/master/GPU_Computing/Dist_GPU_Computing/NCCL_and_CUDA/MultiNode/ncclAllGather_mpi.cu, slurm.schedmd.com/gres.html, github.com/fasrc/User_Codes/blob/master/GPU_Computing/Dist_GPU_Computing/README.md, github.com/fasrc/User_Codes/blob/master/Training/Distributed_GPU_Computing/Exercise4/README.md, github.com/fasrc/User_Codes/blob/master/Training/Distributed_GPU_Computing/Exercise5/README.md, github.com/fasrc/User_Codes/blob/master/Parallel_Computing/R/Large_Data_Processing_R/parallel_computation/R_embarrassingly_parallel.md, github.com/fasrc/User_Codes/blob/master/Parallel_Computing/EP/README.md, github.com/fasrc/User_Codes/blob/master/Applications/Dynamite/README.md

⚠️ Status: PARTIALLY CORRECT (1/2 sources found)

🤖 archi's Answer
The agent attempted to retrieve specific SLURM configuration details and MPI launch instructions for the Cannon cluster, specifically focusing on multi-node setups with one GPU per node. It queried the vector store and local files for examples of `#SBATCH` directives (like `--gpus-per-node`) and `srun` usage. **Summary of Attempt:** The agent gathered information on SLURM job submission scripts and MPI execution commands from the Cannon cluster documentation and example code repositories. **Answer:** To run a multi-node MPI program on Cannon with one GPU per node, you need to configure your SLURM script and launch command as follows: 1. **SLURM Submit Script (`#SBATCH` directives):** * Specify the number of nodes: `#SBATCH --nodes=` * Specify the number of tasks (MPI ranks) per node: `#SBATCH --ntasks-per-node=1` (since you want one rank per node). * Specify one GPU per node: `#SBATCH --gpus-per-node=1` * Ensure you select the correct partition (e.g., `#SBATCH --partition=gpu`). *Example Script:* ```bash #!/bin/bash #SBATCH --job-name=mpi_gpu #SBATCH --nodes=2 #SBATCH --ntasks-per-node=1 #SBATCH --gpus-per-node=1 #SBATCH --time=00:30:00 #SBATCH --partition=gpu module load mpi/cuda/openmpi # Load appropriate MPI module srun ./your_mpi_program ``` 2. **Launching MPI Ranks:** * Use `srun` to launch the MPI executable. SLURM automatically handles the process placement and GPU binding. * Command: `srun ./your_program` * If your program requires explicit MPI initialization (like `MPI_Init`), ensure it is called within the executable launched by `srun`. The environment variables set by SLURM (like `SLURM_NODELIST`, `SLURM_PROCID`) will be available to the MPI ranks. **Note:** This run stopped after hitting the recursion limit of 50.
✅ Expected Answer
In your submit script: choose a GPU-enabled partition (e.g. gpu), set --nodes= with --ntasks-per-node=1 and --gres=gpu:1 (per-node GPU request), load the MPI and CUDA modules with module load. Launch the program with srun (not mpirun directly) so SLURM places one rank per node with the bound GPU; e.g. srun --mpi=pmix ./my_program. Use sinfo and scontrol show partition gpu to confirm node configuration before submitting.
